<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the complete ideal-class packets of maximal orders in totally real sextic fields with no proper intermediate fields become equidistributed, with no escape of mass, in the space of unimodular lattices as their field discriminants tend to infinity. The limit is Haar probability measure. Each packet includes all coordinate-sign translates and is weighted by diagonal-orbit volume.

- **claim-main-equidistribution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $K_i$ be totally real primitive fields of degree six, with $\|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K_i)\|\to\infty$, and choose any ordering $\sigma_i$ of the embeddings of each field. Then $$\int_{X_6} f\,\mathop{}\!d\mu_{K_i,\sigma_i} \longrightarrow\int_{X_6} f\,\mathop{}\!dm_6 \qquad(f\in C_c(X_6)).$$ There is no escape of mass: for every $\epsilon>0$ there is a compact $C\subset X_6$ such that $\mu_{K_i,\sigma_i}(C)\ge1-\epsilon$ for all sufficiently large $i$.
  - Explanation: Complete packets of primitive totally real sextic fields equidistribute to Haar probability on the space of unimodular lattices as discriminants grow, with no escape of mass.
- **claim-packet-volume-unfolding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-packet-volume)): Every orbit in $\mathcal P_{K,\sigma}$ has $A$-volume $(s/2)R_K$, and there are $h_K2^6/s$ distinct orbits. In particular, the total packet volume is $Q\kappa$. For every nonnegative Borel function $f$, $$\int_X\widehat f\,\mathop{}\!d\mu_{K,\sigma} =\frac1{Q\kappa}\sum_{l\ge1}d_K(l)V_f(l/Q), \label{eq:vector-unfolding}$$ where both sides are allowed to be infinite.
  - Explanation: Computes the volume of each orbit and the number of orbits in a packet, and gives the unfolding identity turning packet integrals of lattice sums into sums over ideal norms.
- **claim-uniform-ideal-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ideal-estimate)): For all sufficiently large discriminants $D$ in the present family, $$\begin{aligned} \kappa&\gg (\log D)^{-1}, \label{eq:residue-lower}\\ \sum_{p\le D}\frac{d_K(p)}p &\le \log(\kappa\log D)+O(1), \label{eq:prime-sum}\\ \sum_{x-y<l\le x}d_K(l)&\ll y\kappa \quad\left(D^{1/4}\le x\le D, \ x^{1/2}\le y\le x\right). \label{eq:ideal-interval} \end{aligned}$$ In <a href="#eq:prime-sum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:prime-sum">[eq:prime-sum]</a>, $p$ ranges over rational primes. The constants and the discriminant threshold are uniform in $K$.
  - Explanation: Uniform lower bound for the residue, a bound for the prime sum, and a short-interval bound for ideal counts, all uniform in the field.
- **claim-uniform-cusp-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-cusp)): There are $C>0$ and $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $D$, $$\mu_{K,\sigma}\{x:m(x)\le r\}\le Cr^c \qquad(0<r<1).$$ Consequently the packet probabilities form a tight family as $D\to\infty$, and every weak subsequential limit is an $A$-invariant probability measure on $X$.
  - Explanation: Packet mass near the cusp is bounded by a power of the radius, giving tightness and invariance of weak limits.
- **claim-ball-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ball)): Suppose $D_i\to\infty$ and $\mu_i$ converges weakly to a probability $\mu$. For every compact $\Omega\subset X$, there are constants $C_\Omega,r_\Omega>0$ such that $$\mu(xB(r))\le C_\Omega r^6 \qquad(x\in\Omega,\ 0<r<r_\Omega).$$
  - Explanation: Any probability limit of the packets gives small balls about compact sets a mass of the order of the sixth power of the radius.
- **claim-trace-slice-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-slice)): The lattice $\mathcal L_K$ has Euclidean covolume $\sqrt6/Q$ in $H$ and admits a fundamental parallelotope of diameter $O(D^{-1/30})$. Let $S$ be a nonempty proper subset of $\{1,\ldots,6\}$. For fixed $M>0$, uniformly in $m\in\mathbb Z$ and intervals $J\subset\mathbb R$ of length $\delta>0$, $$\#\left\{z\in\mathfrak D_K^{-1}: \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{K/\mathbb Q}z=m, \ \|\sigma_j(z)\|\le M\ (1\le j\le6), \ \sum_{j\in S}\sigma_j(z)\in J\right\} \ll_M Q\bigl(\delta+D^{-1/30}\bigr). \label{eq:trace-slice-count}$$
  - Explanation: The trace slice of the codifferent is a lattice with explicit covolume and small cells, so thin slices contain few codifferent elements.
- **claim-two-sided-tube-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-bowen)): Let $S$ be a nonempty proper subset of $\{1,\ldots,6\}$, and let $b=(b_1,\ldots,b_6)\in L$ satisfy $$\|b_j-b_k\|\ge1\qquad(j\in S,\ k\notin S).$$ For every compact $\Omega\subset X$, there is an identity neighborhood $O\subset G$ such that, for all sufficiently large $i$, all $x\in\Omega$, and all $T_{-,i}\le t\le T_{+,i}$, $$\mu_i\bigl(x(a(tb)Oa(-tb)\cap a(-tb)Oa(tb))\bigr) \le e^{-t/3}. \label{eq:bowen-bound}$$ The neighborhood and discriminant threshold may be chosen simultaneously for any fixed finite collection of pairs $(S,b)$.
  - Explanation: Packet mass of two-sided diagonal tubes decays exponentially over a growing time interval when the diagonal element separates a cut of coordinates.
- **claim-positive-entropy-components** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-positive-entropy)): Let $\mu$ be an $A$-invariant probability on $X$. Suppose that, for every compact $\Omega\subset X$, there are $C_\Omega,r_\Omega>0$ such that $$\label{eq:ordinary-ball-hypothesis} \mu(xB(r))\le C_\Omega r^n \quad(x\in\Omega,\ 0<r<r_\Omega),$$ where $B(r)$ is a matrix neighborhood of the identity of radius $r$. Every $A$-invariant probability $\xi\le c\mu$, with $c<\infty$, satisfies $h_\xi(T)\ge\lambda/3$. Consequently almost every $A$-ergodic component of $\mu$ has positive entropy for $T$.
  - Explanation: The ball bound forces positive entropy on every dominated invariant probability and hence on almost every ergodic component.
- **claim-ekl-classification** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-ekl)): For $n\ge3$, an $A$-invariant, $A$-ergodic probability on $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb R)$ that has positive entropy for some diagonal one-parameter subgroup is the invariant probability on a closed finite-volume orbit $xJ$, where $J$ is a closed connected subgroup containing $A$.
  - Explanation: Positive-entropy ergodic invariant probabilities are homogeneous measures on closed finite-volume orbits.
- **claim-block-groups** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-block-groups)): Let $J$ be a connected closed subgroup of $G$ containing $A$, and suppose $xJ$ carries a finite nonzero $J$-invariant measure. There is a partition $\{1,\ldots,n\}=B_1\sqcup\cdots\sqcup B_d$ such that $J$ is the identity component of the determinant-one block-diagonal group for this partition.
  - Explanation: Connected subgroups containing the diagonal group that carry finite invariant measures on an orbit are block-diagonal.
- **claim-fixed-pieces** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-pieces)): Every proper orbit $xJ$ in Theorem <a href="#thm:ekl" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:ekl">9</a> is contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{Fix}}(a)=\{y\in X:ya=y\}$ for some nonidentity $a\in A$ whose entries are algebraic numbers. There are only countably many possible matrices $a$. For each such $a$, its fixed set is covered by countably many smooth pieces. Whenever $a_{jj}\ne a_{kk}$, the direction of $U_{jk}=\{1+tE_{jk}:t\in\mathbb R\}$ is nowhere tangent to these pieces, and each piece meets a sufficiently small $U_{jk}$ plaque in at most one point.
  - Explanation: Proper homogeneous orbits lie in countably many smooth fixed-point pieces of algebraic diagonal matrices, each meeting root plaques in at most one point.
- **claim-no-periods** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-no-periods)): Let $\nu$ be an $A$-invariant probability on $X$ and let $V$ be a pattern group. For $\nu$-almost every $x$, $$xv=x,\quad v\in V \quad\Longrightarrow\quad v=1.$$ At every such $x$, the image $xK$ of any prescribed compact displacement range $K\subset V$ is contained in an embedded plaque with a transverse neighborhood. Consequently, for any compact displacement range in $V$ and any $\epsilon>0$, finitely many inner plaque charts cover a set of $\nu$-measure at least $1-\epsilon$, and their outer charts contain that range about every point of the corresponding inner charts.
  - Explanation: Almost every point has no nontrivial pattern-group period, so displacement ranges embed as plaques with charts extending past them.
- **claim-common-plateau** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-plateau)): After passage to a subsequence there are integer depths $l_{-,i}$ and $l_{+,i}$ such that $$\label{eq:plateau-room} T_-\prec l_-\prec l_+\prec T_+.$$ For every chart and every fixed finite plaque-bin partition in the countable stock, the expected $\ell^1$ distance between the conditional bin-probability vectors at depths $l_-$ and $l_+$ tends to zero, averaged also over a uniform shift of the nested dyadic grid. The same conclusion holds between any two intervening depths for that grid.* *The shifts can be selected so that these conclusions hold for every fixed chart and fixed bin partition and so that random-grid boundary estimates hold at the two endpoint depths and at any prescribed countable collection of interior depth sequences. When coordinate usages share transverse labels, their grids may share the corresponding shifts. At interior depths, conditional bin probabilities are unchanged in expected $\ell^1$ distance by using auxiliary shifted grids selected with the same boundary convention.
  - Explanation: A martingale argument yields a range of transverse depths over which conditional plaque laws barely change.
- **claim-sampling-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-sampling)): In any fixed chart, conditional on its transverse coordinate $q$ and its own limiting probability $P^{\mathcal B}$, the sampled plaque coordinate $h$ has law $P^{\mathcal B}$. In particular, $h\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}P^{\mathcal B}$ almost surely on that chart. This assertion conditions on this one probability, rather than on the entire augmented state.
  - Explanation: Given its transverse coordinate and its own limiting probability, the sampled plaque point has exactly that law.
- **claim-patch-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-patch-comparison)): In the preceding chart, let $p$ range over pairwise disjoint measurable patches, each lying transversely in one coarse cell $E(p)$. Suppose a subset of each patch has the product form $$p^{\mathrm{in}}= \bigcup_{e\in I_p}\bigl(e\times B_p\bigr), \qquad B_p=\bigcup_{j\in J(p)}J_j,$$ and the sum of the discarded masses $\mu_i(p\setminus p^{\mathrm{in}})$ is at most $\epsilon$. The collections $I_p,J(p)$ may depend on $i,p$, and the number of patches need not be bounded. If $L_p$ is the plaque-bin law conditional on $p$, then, with errors weighted by $\mu_i(p)$, $$L_p\quad\hbox{agrees with}\quad \frac{\mathbf 1_{B_p}P_{E(p)}}{P_{E(p)}(B_p)} \quad\text{to total expected }\ell^1\text{ error } O(\epsilon)+o(1).$$ An arbitrary law is used if the denominator vanishes. The unnormalized version is $$\label{eq:patch-unnormalized} \sum_p\mu_i(p) \left\\|P_{E(p)}(B_p)L_p- \mathbf 1_{B_p}P_{E(p)}\right\\|_1 \le O(\epsilon)+o(1).$$ If patches and a disjoint refinement into subpatches admit such trims with the same coarse cell and the same plaque-bin union, their conditional bin laws agree with one another with the corresponding weighted error.* *These assertions also hold for bounded continuous tests, up to their uniform continuity error on the fixed bins. A test may depend on the sampled point, provided its norm and continuity modulus in the tested plaque variable are uniform. At the output sample, $P_{E(p)}$ may be replaced in fixed-bin expressions by the recorded data probability, with an additional error tending to zero.
  - Explanation: Conditional laws on trimmed patches agree with the coarse conditional law restricted to the admitted bins, with small weighted error.
- **claim-plaque-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-plaque-comparison)): For the augmented limit constructed above the following assertions hold.* 1. *For every pattern group $V$, its centered probabilities in different charts agree projectively on their common plaque sheet near the sampled point. They extend to a measurable nonzero projective locally finite measure $m^V_\omega$ on $V$, with $1\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}m^V_\omega$. The chart probabilities represent its restrictions on all bounded displacement ranges contained in their plaque domains.* 2. *Fix $b\in L$. In every subsequential joint limit of $$\bigl(Z_i(x),Z_i(xa(b))\bigr),\qquad x\sim\mu_i,$$ the two $V$ measures are related projectively by the pushforward under $v\mapsto a(-b)va(b)$.* 3. *Fix a root $\beta$ and an interior time scale $R$ with room for interior depths on both sides. For every fixed $C<\infty$ and every sequence $$b_i\in\ker\beta,\qquad \\|b_i\\|\le CR_i,$$ the measures $m^{U_\beta}$ in every subsequential joint limit of $\bigl(Z_i(x),Z_i(xa(b_i))\bigr)$ agree projectively. This assertion holds for every such sequence $b_i$; the defining grids and state construction are unchanged.
  - Explanation: Plaque data in different charts agree projectively, define a locally finite measure on the pattern group, and are equivariant under fixed and wall-parallel diagonal motions.
- **claim-product-rule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-product)): Let $V$ and $W$ be pattern groups, let $U=U_\alpha$ be a one-dimensional root group, and suppose that multiplication gives a coordinate decomposition $$V=WU,\qquad W\mathrel{\triangleleft}V.$$ Suppose also that some $a_0\in A$ fixes $U$ by conjugation and that the automorphism $$\Delta(w)=a_0^{-1}wa_0$$ strictly contracts $W$. Then, for almost every augmented state $\omega$, $$\label{eq:product-rule} m^V_\omega \ \propto\ ( (w,u)\longmapsto wu)_* \bigl(m^W_\omega\otimes m^U_\omega\bigr).$$ Here $\propto$ denotes equality up to multiplication by a positive constant.
  - Explanation: When a diagonal element fixes a root group and contracts a normal pattern subgroup, the limiting plaque measure is the product of the two factors.
- **claim-u-factor** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-u-factor)): In each chart <a href="#eq:simultaneous-product-chart" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:simultaneous-product-chart">[eq:simultaneous-product-chart]</a>, the limiting $V$-plaque probability is a product in the coordinates $w,u$. Its $u$ marginal agrees with the $U$-plaque probability at the sampled state. Consequently there is a projective locally finite measure $\nu^W_\omega$ on $W$ such that $$\label{eq:provisional-product} m^V_\omega \ \propto\ ((w,u)\longmapsto wu)_* (\nu^W_\omega\otimes m^U_\omega).$$
  - Explanation: Contraction shows the root coordinate is independent of the normal factor and has the root plaque law as marginal.
- **claim-adapted-tiles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-tiles)): There is a compact set $\mathcal F\subset W$, of Haar measure one and with Haar-null boundary, such that the translates $\gamma\mathcal F$, $\gamma\in\Gamma_W$, tile $W$ with disjoint interiors. Moreover, $$\mathcal F =\bigcup_{d\in\mathcal D}\Delta(d)\Delta(\mathcal F), \qquad \mathcal D= \{\gamma\in\Gamma_W:0\le \gamma_{jk}<2^{d_{jk}} \text{ for each pattern entry }jk\},$$ where $d_{jk}$ is the contraction weight in <a href="#eq:integer-contraction" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:integer-contraction">[eq:integer-contraction]</a>. The union is disjoint off boundaries.* *For every integer $k\ge0$, the tiles $$\gamma\Delta^k\mathcal F,\qquad \gamma\in\Delta^k\Gamma_W,$$ form a subdivision, and these subdivisions are nested as $k$ increases. They may all be shifted by the same left translation $g$. If $g$ is distributed according to Haar probability on $W/\Gamma_W$, then, for every fixed $w\in W$, its coordinate in its shifted level-$k$ tile has uniform Haar distribution on $\mathcal F$, after rescaling by $\Delta^{-k}$. In particular, the probability of lying in a fixed $\rho$-collar of $\partial\mathcal F$ in these coordinates tends to zero as $\rho\downarrow0$, uniformly in $k$ and $w$.
  - Explanation: There is a self-replicating tile adapted to the contraction whose translates tile the group, with nested subdivisions and vanishing boundary collar probability.
- **claim-stage-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stage-information)): Fix a chart <a href="#eq:simultaneous-product-chart" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:simultaneous-product-chart">[eq:simultaneous-product-chart]</a> and integers $F,S\ge1$. Let $Q_F$ be its depth-$F$ $q$-cell label, and let $U_S$ be its depth-$S$ $u$-cell label. Let $K_k$ be the level-$k$ tile label of $w$, with any common shift as in Lemma <a href="#lem:tiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:tiles">19</a>, restricted to the chart. For every pair of integers $T\ge j\ge1$, $$\label{eq:stage-information} \sum_{k=0}^{T-j} I_{\mu_i}(K_{k+j};U_S\mid Q_F,K_k) \le Cj(1+S).$$ Here the measure in the chart is normalized for defining entropy; its mass is restored when errors are integrated over the chart. The constant $C$ depends only on the fixed coordinate domain, and is independent of $i,F,S,T,j$, the sampling measure, and the tile shift. Consequently, along any sequence with $T\to\infty$ and $S/T\to0$, for every fixed $j$, averaged over $T/2\le k\le3T/4$, revealing $U_S$ changes the conditional law of $K_{k+j}$, given $(Q_F,K_k)$, by $o(1)$ in expected $\ell^1$ distance. The same assertions hold after averaging over shifts.
  - Explanation: The total information a transverse label carries about tile subdivisions over many stages is bounded, so revealing it changes the tile law negligibly.
- **claim-activity-composition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-activity)): Almost surely, activity is transitively closed: $$a_{pq}(\omega)a_{qr}(\omega) \bigl(1-a_{pr}(\omega)\bigr)=0 \qquad(p,q,r\text{ distinct}).$$ Let $b\in L$ be regular, let $V$ be the pattern group of all $b$-positive roots, and let $V_\omega^+$ be the subgroup generated by the active $b$-positive root groups. These active roots form a pattern, and $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}m^V_\omega\subset V_\omega^+.$$ Both conclusions hold simultaneously for every regular $b$.
  - Explanation: Active roots are transitively closed and the plaque measure is supported on the group generated by active positive roots.
- **claim-va-support** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-va-support)): With $V$ and $V_\omega^+$ as in Proposition <a href="#prop:activity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:activity">21</a>, the limiting $VA$-plaque probability, written in displacement coordinates from its sampled point, is supported on $V_\omega^+A$ within its chart.
  - Explanation: The plaque probability enlarged by the diagonal group is supported on the active pattern group times the diagonal group.
- **claim-activity-indicators** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-activity-indicators)): Fix an interior scale $R_i$ allowed in the long-wall comparison of Proposition <a href="#prop:plaque-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:plaque-comparison">16</a>, with ratio-separated depths on both sides of it inside $(l_{-,i},l_{+,i})$. There exist measurable functions $g_{jk,i}:X\to\{0,1\}$ such that $$\operatorname{Law}_{\mu_i}\bigl(Z_i(x),(g_{jk,i}(x))_{j\ne k}\bigr) \ \Longrightarrow\ \operatorname{Law}_{\mathsf P} \bigl(\omega,(a_{jk}(\omega))_{j\ne k}\bigr).$$ They satisfy asymptotic transitivity: $$\int_X g_{pq,i}(x)g_{qr,i}(x) \bigl(1-g_{pr,i}(x)\bigr)\,d\mu_i(x)=o(1) \qquad(p,q,r\text{ distinct}),$$ and, for every fixed $C<\infty$, $$\label{eq:wall-invariance} \sup_{\substack{b\in\ker\beta_{jk}\\ \\|b\\|\le CR_i}} \int_X\bigl\|g_{jk,i}(xa(b))-g_{jk,i}(x)\bigr\|\,d\mu_i(x) \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Measurable indicators along the sequence converge jointly to the activity indicators, are asymptotically transitive and nearly invariant along walls.
- **claim-transverse-inactive** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transverse-inactive)): Let $S\subset X$ be a Borel set whose intersection with each local $U_{jk}$ plaque is countable. Then $$a_{jk}(\omega)=0 \quad\text{for $\mathsf P$-almost every state with }x\in S.$$ In particular, this applies to the fixed-point pieces of Lemma <a href="#lem:fixed-pieces" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:fixed-pieces">11</a> for roots crossing distinct eigenvalues: each such piece meets a sufficiently small root plaque in at most one point. It also applies to countable unions of these pieces.
  - Explanation: A set meeting root plaques in countably many points forces the root to be inactive on states over it.
- **claim-segment-activity** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-segment-activity)): For $\delta$ sufficiently small, the preceding choices have the following property. For every oriented cut $J$, every $C<\infty$, every $\tau>0$, and every sequence of probabilities $\xi_i$ on $X$ satisfying $\xi_i\leq C\mu_i$, $$\label{eq:segment-activity} \liminf_{i\to\infty}\frac1{R_i} \int_0^{\tau R_i}\int_X A_{J,i}\bigl(xa(tb_J)\bigr)\,\mathop{}\!d\xi_i(x)\,\mathop{}\!dt>0.$$ The same assertion holds after passage to any subsequence.
  - Explanation: Crossing-root activity averaged along long diagonal segments stays positive for any dominated sequence of starting probabilities.
- **claim-entropy-partition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-partition)): Given a sufficiently small identity neighborhood $O\subset G$, there is a countable Borel partition $\mathcal P$ of $X$ with the following properties.* 1. *Every atom has $\mu$-null boundary and lies in an inner part of a relatively compact $VA$ plaque chart from the fixed stock. The chart extends beyond the atom with a margin.* 2. *If $x,y$ have the same symbols at times $0,\ldots,N$, there are relative lifts $h_t\in O$, $0\leq t\leq N$, such that $$ya(tb)=xa(tb)h_t, \qquad h_{t+1}=a(-b)h_ta(b).$$* 3. *For every fixed $C$, the entropies $H_\nu(\mathcal P)$ are bounded uniformly for all sufficiently large $i$ and all probabilities $\nu\leq C\mu_i$. Moreover, there are finite coarsenings $\mathcal P^{(q)}$, obtained by keeping finitely many atoms and combining the others into one atom, for which $$\label{eq:partition-entropy-tails} \lim_{q\to\infty}\limsup_{i\to\infty} \sup_{\nu\leq C\mu_i} H_\nu(\mathcal P\mid\mathcal P^{(q)})=0.$$
  - Explanation: A countable partition adapted to plaque charts gives consistent orbit names with uniformly bounded entropy and vanishing tails.
- **claim-inactive-cell-concentration** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-inactive-cell-concentration)): Fix finitely many atoms $S$ of $\mathcal P$, with their $VA$ charts, and an interior transverse depth $l_i\prec R_i$. Suppose that probabilities $\nu_i\leq C\mu_i$ satisfy $\int A_{J,i}\,\mathop{}\!d\nu_i\to0$. Condition on one of these symbols $S$ and on its transverse $q$ cell. Draw two points independently from the resulting conditional probability of $\nu_i$. Then their $z$ coordinates differ by a quantity tending to zero in probability, averaged with the probabilities of the conditioning events.* *The conclusion also holds on average over any finite list of such probabilities, with arbitrary probability weights, if their weighted mean of $\int A_{J,i}\,\mathop{}\!d\nu_i$ tends to zero.
  - Explanation: If crossing activity vanishes on average, conditional laws within a cell concentrate in the cross-root coordinate.
- **claim-full-activity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-full-activity)): For every ordered pair $j\ne k$ in $\{1,\ldots,6\}$, $$\label{eq:full-activity} \int_X g_{jk,i}\,\mathop{}\!d\mu_i\longrightarrow1.$$ Consequently every root is active almost surely in the limiting state law.
  - Explanation: Every root indicator tends to one in mean, so every root is active almost surely in the limit.
- **claim-root-line-laws** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-root-line-laws)): For any such convergent subsequence, the limiting law and its prelimit arrays have the following properties.* 1. *Almost surely, for every $v_0\in L$ with rational coordinates, every oriented cut $J$, and every positive rational $\tau$, $$\label{eq:positive-cut-integral} \sum_{\beta\in\mathcal E_J}\int_0^\tau F_\beta\bigl(\beta(v_0+t b_J)\bigr)\,\mathop{}\!dt>0.$$* 2. *For distinct $j,k,\ell$, and every bounded rectangle $B\subset\mathbb R^2$, the nonnegative defects satisfy $$\label{eq:line-transitivity-defect} \mathbb E_{x\sim\mu_i}\int_B G_{jk,i}^{x}(s)G_{k\ell,i}^{x}(t) \bigl(1-G_{j\ell,i}^{x}(s+t)\bigr)\,\mathop{}\!ds\,\mathop{}\!dt \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: Along root lines the activity functions converge in law, with positive cut integrals and vanishing transitivity defects on rectangles.
- **claim-deterministic-root-arrays** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-deterministic-root-arrays)): If <a href="#eq:full-activity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:full-activity">[eq:full-activity]</a> fails for some root, there is a subsequence and a deterministic choice of arrays $(G_{\beta,i})_\beta$, each taken from <a href="#eq:root-line-functions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:root-line-functions">[eq:root-line-functions]</a>, with the following properties:* 1. *$G_{\beta,i}\to F_\beta$ weak-\* on bounded intervals, and the deterministic limiting functions satisfy <a href="#eq:positive-cut-integral" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:positive-cut-integral">[eq:positive-cut-integral]</a> for all its stated parameters;* 2. *every defect in <a href="#eq:line-transitivity-defect" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:line-transitivity-defect">[eq:line-transitivity-defect]</a>, without its expectation, tends to zero on every bounded rectangle;* 3. *for some root $\beta_0$ and some bounded interval $I$ of positive length, $$\label{eq:limiting-deficiency} \int_I(1-F_{\beta_0}(s))\,\mathop{}\!ds>0.$$
  - Explanation: If full activity fails, deterministic arrays exist with vanishing defects and a positive deficiency for some root.
- **claim-positive-root-intervals** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-root-intervals)): For every root $\beta$ and every nonempty open interval $I\subset\mathbb R$, $$\int_I F_\beta(s)\,\mathop{}\!ds>0.$$
  - Explanation: The limiting root function has positive integral on every nonempty open interval, via connectivity of the root graph.
- **claim-convolution-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-convolution)): Fix $B,M,v,L>0$. Consider the probability densities $p$ on $\mathbb R$ such that $$\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}p\subset[-B,B],\qquad 0\leq p\leq M, \qquad \int x p(x)\,\mathop{}\!dx=0, \qquad \int x^2p(x)\,\mathop{}\!dx\geq v.$$ There are an integer $m\geq2$ and $c>0$, depending only on $B,M,v,L$, such that $$p^{*m}(x)\geq c\qquad(\|x\|\leq L)$$ for every such density. The inequality uses the continuous representative of the convolution density.
  - Explanation: A fixed convolution power of any bounded centered density with variance bounded below is uniformly bounded below on a compact interval.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bowen",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-plateau",
        "exact_quote": "After passage to a subsequence there are integer depths $l_{-,i}$ and $l_{+,i}$ such that $$\\label{eq:plateau-room}\n T_-\\prec l_-\\prec l_+\\prec T_+.$$ For every chart and every fixed finite plaque-bin partition in the countable stock, the expected $\\ell^1$ distance between the conditional bin-probability vectors at depths $l_-$ and $l_+$ tends to zero, averaged also over a uniform shift of the nested dyadic grid. The same conclusion holds between any two intervening depths for that grid.*\n\n*The shifts can be selected so that these conclusions hold for every fixed chart and fixed bin partition and so that random-grid boundary estimates hold at the two endpoint depths and at any prescribed countable collection of interior depth sequences. When coordinate usages share transverse labels, their grids may share the corresponding shifts. At interior depths, conditional bin probabilities are unchanged in expected $\\ell^1$ distance by using auxiliary shifted grids selected with the same boundary convention.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-plateau",
          "exact_quote": "After passage to a subsequence there are integer depths $l_{-,i}$ and $l_{+,i}$ such that $$\\label{eq:plateau-room}\n T_-\\prec l_-\\prec l_+\\prec T_+.$$ For every chart and every fixed finite plaque-bin partition in the countable stock, the expected $\\ell^1$ distance between the conditional bin-probability vectors at depths $l_-$ and $l_+$ tends to zero, averaged also over a uniform shift of the nested dyadic grid. The same conclusion holds between any two intervening depths for that grid.*\n\n*The shifts can be selected so that these conclusions hold for every fixed chart and fixed bin partition and so that random-grid boundary estimates hold at the two endpoint depths and at any prescribed countable collection of interior depth sequences. When coordinate usages share transverse labels, their grids may share the corresponding shifts. At interior depths, conditional bin probabilities are unchanged in expected $\\ell^1$ distance by using auxiliary shifted grids selected with the same boundary convention.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "After passage to a subsequence there are integer depths $l_{-,i}$ and $l_{+,i}$ such that $$\\label{eq:plateau-room}\n T_-\\prec l_-\\prec l_+\\prec T_+.$$ For every chart and every fixed finite plaque-bin partition in the countable stock, the expected $\\ell^1$ distance between the conditional bin-probability vectors at depths $l_-$ and $l_+$ tends to zero, averaged also over a uniform shift of the nested dyadic grid. The same conclusion holds between any two intervening depths for that grid.*\n\n*The shifts can be selected so that these conclusions hold for every fixed chart and fixed bin partition and so that random-grid boundary estimates hold at the two endpoint depths and at any prescribed countable collection of interior depth sequences. When coordinate usages share transverse labels, their grids may share the corresponding shifts. At interior depths, conditional bin probabilities are unchanged in expected $\\ell^1$ distance by using auxiliary shifted grids selected with the same boundary convention.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-sampling",
        "exact_quote": "In any fixed chart, conditional on its transverse coordinate $q$ and its own limiting probability $P^{\\mathcal B}$, the sampled plaque coordinate $h$ has law $P^{\\mathcal B}$. In particular, $h\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}P^{\\mathcal B}$ almost surely on that chart. This assertion conditions on this one probability, rather than on the entire augmented state.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.3"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-015.3"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-patch-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "In the preceding chart, let $p$ range over pairwise disjoint measurable patches, each lying transversely in one coarse cell $E(p)$. Suppose a subset of each patch has the product form $$p^{\\mathrm{in}}=\n \\bigcup_{e\\in I_p}\\bigl(e\\times B_p\\bigr),\n \\qquad B_p=\\bigcup_{j\\in J(p)}J_j,$$ and the sum of the discarded masses $\\mu_i(p\\setminus p^{\\mathrm{in}})$ is at most $\\epsilon$. The collections $I_p,J(p)$ may depend on $i,p$, and the number of patches need not be bounded. If $L_p$ is the plaque-bin law conditional on $p$, then, with errors weighted by $\\mu_i(p)$, $$L_p\\quad\\hbox{agrees with}\\quad\n \\frac{\\mathbf 1_{B_p}P_{E(p)}}{P_{E(p)}(B_p)}\n \\quad\\text{to total expected }\\ell^1\\text{ error }\n O(\\epsilon)+o(1).$$ An arbitrary law is used if the denominator vanishes. The unnormalized version is $$\\label{eq:patch-unnormalized}\n \\sum_p\\mu_i(p)\n \\left\\|P_{E(p)}(B_p)L_p-\n             \\mathbf 1_{B_p}P_{E(p)}\\right\\|_1\n \\le O(\\epsilon)+o(1).$$ If patches and a disjoint refinement into subpatches admit such trims with the same coarse cell and the same plaque-bin union, their conditional bin laws agree with one another with the corresponding weighted error.*\n\n*These assertions also hold for bounded continuous tests, up to their uniform continuity error on the fixed bins. A test may depend on the sampled point, provided its norm and continuity modulus in the tested plaque variable are uniform. At the output sample, $P_{E(p)}$ may be replaced in fixed-bin expressions by the recorded data probability, with an additional error tending to zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.3"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-plaque-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "For the augmented limit constructed above the following assertions hold.*\n\n1.  *For every pattern group $V$, its centered probabilities in different charts agree projectively on their common plaque sheet near the sampled point. They extend to a measurable nonzero projective locally finite measure $m^V_\\omega$ on $V$, with $1\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}m^V_\\omega$. The chart probabilities represent its restrictions on all bounded displacement ranges contained in their plaque domains.*\n\n2.  *Fix $b\\in L$. In every subsequential joint limit of $$\\bigl(Z_i(x),Z_i(xa(b))\\bigr),\\qquad x\\sim\\mu_i,$$ the two $V$ measures are related projectively by the pushforward under $v\\mapsto a(-b)va(b)$.*\n\n3.  *Fix a root $\\beta$ and an interior time scale $R$ with room for interior depths on both sides. For every fixed $C<\\infty$ and every sequence $$b_i\\in\\ker\\beta,\\qquad \\|b_i\\|\\le CR_i,$$ the measures $m^{U_\\beta}$ in every subsequential joint limit of $\\bigl(Z_i(x),Z_i(xa(b_i))\\bigr)$ agree projectively. This assertion holds for every such sequence $b_i$; the defining grids and state construction are unchanged.",

      },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-plaque-comparison",

For every fixed $C<\\infty$ and every sequence $$b_i\\in\\ker\\beta,\\qquad \\|b_i\\|\\le CR_i,$$ the measures $m^{U_\\beta}$ in every subsequential joint limit of $\\bigl(Z_i(x),Z_i(xa(b_i))\\bigr)$ agree projectively.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-product",
        "exact_quote": "Let $V$ and $W$ be pattern groups, let $U=U_\\alpha$ be a one-dimensional root group, and suppose that multiplication gives a coordinate decomposition $$V=WU,\\qquad W\\mathrel{\\triangleleft}V.$$ Suppose also that some $a_0\\in A$ fixes $U$ by conjugation and that the automorphism $$\\Delta(w)=a_0^{-1}wa_0$$ strictly contracts $W$. Then, for almost every augmented state $\\omega$, $$\\label{eq:product-rule}\n m^V_\\omega\n   \\ \\propto\\\n   ( (w,u)\\longmapsto wu)_*\n          \\bigl(m^W_\\omega\\otimes m^U_\\omega\\bigr).$$ Here $\\propto$ denotes equality up to multiplication by a positive constant.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-product",
          "exact_quote": "Let $V$ and $W$ be pattern groups, let $U=U_\\alpha$ be a one-dimensional root group, and suppose that multiplication gives a coordinate decomposition $$V=WU,\\qquad W\\mathrel{\\triangleleft}V.$$ Suppose also that some $a_0\\in A$ fixes $U$ by conjugation and that the automorphism $$\\Delta(w)=a_0^{-1}wa_0$$ strictly contracts $W$. Then, for almost every augmented state $\\omega$, $$\\label{eq:product-rule}\n m^V_\\omega\n   \\ \\propto\\\n   ( (w,u)\\longmapsto wu)_*\n          \\bigl(m^W_\\omega\\otimes m^U_\\omega\\bigr).$$ Here $\\propto$ denotes equality up to multiplication by a positive constant.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.3"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $V$ and $W$ be pattern groups, let $U=U_\\alpha$ be a one-dimensional root group, and suppose that multiplication gives a coordinate decomposition $$V=WU,\\qquad W\\mathrel{\\triangleleft}V.$$ Suppose also that some $a_0\\in A$ fixes $U$ by conjugation and that the automorphism $$\\Delta(w)=a_0^{-1}wa_0$$ strictly contracts $W$. Then, for almost every augmented state $\\omega$, $$\\label{eq:product-rule}\n m^V_\\omega\n   \\ \\propto\\\n   ( (w,u)\\longmapsto wu)_*\n          \\bigl(m^W_\\omega\\otimes m^U_\\omega\\bigr).$$ Here $\\propto$ denotes equality up to multiplication by a positive constant.",
      "explanation": "When a diagonal element fixes a root group and contracts a normal pattern subgroup, the limiting plaque measure is the product of the two factors.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-product",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-u-factor",
        "exact_quote": "In each chart <a href=\"#eq:simultaneous-product-chart\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:simultaneous-product-chart\">[eq:simultaneous-product-chart]</a>, the limiting $V$-plaque probability is a product in the coordinates $w,u$. Its $u$ marginal agrees with the $U$-plaque probability at the sampled state. Consequently there is a projective locally finite measure $\\nu^W_\\omega$ on $W$ such that $$\\label{eq:provisional-product}\n m^V_\\omega\n \\ \\propto\\\n ((w,u)\\longmapsto wu)_*\n          (\\nu^W_\\omega\\otimes m^U_\\omega).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-015.3"
      },
      "evidence": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tiles",
        "exact_quote": "There is a compact set $\\mathcal F\\subset W$, of Haar measure one and with Haar-null boundary, such that the translates $\\gamma\\mathcal F$, $\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_W$, tile $W$ with disjoint interiors. Moreover, $$\\mathcal F\n    =\\bigcup_{d\\in\\mathcal D}\\Delta(d)\\Delta(\\mathcal F),\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal D=\n \\{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_W:0\\le \\gamma_{jk}<2^{d_{jk}}\n                         \\text{ for each pattern entry }jk\\},$$ where $d_{jk}$ is the contraction weight in <a href=\"#eq:integer-contraction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-contraction\">[eq:integer-contraction]</a>. The union is disjoint off boundaries.*\n\n*For every integer $k\\ge0$, the tiles $$\\gamma\\Delta^k\\mathcal F,\\qquad\n       \\gamma\\in\\Delta^k\\Gamma_W,$$ form a subdivision, and these subdivisions are nested as $k$ increases. They may all be shifted by the same left translation $g$. If $g$ is distributed according to Haar probability on $W/\\Gamma_W$, then, for every fixed $w\\in W$, its coordinate in its shifted level-$k$ tile has uniform Haar distribution on $\\mathcal F$, after rescaling by $\\Delta^{-k}$. In particular, the probability of lying in a fixed $\\rho$-collar of $\\partial\\mathcal F$ in these coordinates tends to zero as $\\rho\\downarrow0$, uniformly in $k$ and $w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-015.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tiles",
          "exact_quote": "There is a compact set $\\mathcal F\\subset W$, of Haar measure one and with Haar-null boundary, such that the translates $\\gamma\\mathcal F$, $\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_W$, tile $W$ with disjoint interiors. Moreover, $$\\mathcal F\n    =\\bigcup_{d\\in\\mathcal D}\\Delta(d)\\Delta(\\mathcal F),\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal D=\n \\{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_W:0\\le \\gamma_{jk}<2^{d_{jk}}\n                         \\text{ for each pattern entry }jk\\},$$ where $d_{jk}$ is the contraction weight in <a href=\"#eq:integer-contraction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-contraction\">[eq:integer-contraction]</a>. The union is disjoint off boundaries.*\n\n*For every integer $k\\ge0$, the tiles $$\\gamma\\Delta^k\\mathcal F,\\qquad\n       \\gamma\\in\\Delta^k\\Gamma_W,$$ form a subdivision, and these subdivisions are nested as $k$ increases. They may all be shifted by the same left translation $g$. If $g$ is distributed according to Haar probability on $W/\\Gamma_W$, then, for every fixed $w\\in W$, its coordinate in its shifted level-$k$ tile has uniform Haar distribution on $\\mathcal F$, after rescaling by $\\Delta^{-k}$. In particular, the probability of lying in a fixed $\\rho$-collar of $\\partial\\mathcal F$ in these coordinates tends to zero as $\\rho\\downarrow0$, uniformly in $k$ and $w$.",
          "source_id": "PM-015.3"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There is a compact set $\\mathcal F\\subset W$, of Haar measure one and with Haar-null boundary, such that the translates $\\gamma\\mathcal F$, $\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_W$, tile $W$ with disjoint interiors. Moreover, $$\\mathcal F\n    =\\bigcup_{d\\in\\mathcal D}\\Delta(d)\\Delta(\\mathcal F),\n \\qquad\n \\mathcal D=\n \\{\\gamma\\in\\Gamma_W:0\\le \\gamma_{jk}<2^{d_{jk}}\n                         \\text{ for each pattern entry }jk\\},$$ where $d_{jk}$ is the contraction weight in <a href=\"#eq:integer-contraction\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:integer-contraction\">[eq:integer-contraction]</a>. The union is disjoint off boundaries.*\n\n*For every integer $k\\ge0$, the tiles $$\\gamma\\Delta^k\\mathcal F,\\qquad\n       \\gamma\\in\\Delta^k\\Gamma_W,$$ form a subdivision, and these subdivisions are nested as $k$ increases. They may all be shifted by the same left translation $g$. In particular, the probability of lying in a fixed $\\rho$-collar of $\\partial\\mathcal F$ in these coordinates tends to zero as $\\rho\\downarrow0$, uniformly in $k$ and $w$.",
      "explanation": "There is a self-replicating tile adapted to the contraction whose translates tile the group, with nested subdivisions and vanishing boundary collar probability.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "id": "claim-adapted-tiles",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stage-information",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a chart <a href=\"#eq:simultaneous-product-chart\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:simultaneous-product-chart\">[eq:simultaneous-product-chart]</a> and integers $F,S\\ge1$. Let $Q_F$ be its depth-$F$ $q$-cell label, and let $U_S$ be its depth-$S$ $u$-cell label. Let $K_k$ be the level-$k$ tile label of $w$, with any common shift as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:tiles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:tiles\">19</a>, restricted to the chart. For every pair of integers $T\\ge j\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:stage-information}\n \\sum_{k=0}^{T-j}\n       I_{\\mu_i}(K_{k+j};U_S\\mid Q_F,K_k)\n                           \\le Cj(1+S).$$ Here the measure in the chart is normalized for defining entropy; its mass is restored when errors are integrated over the chart. The constant $C$ depends only on the fixed coordinate domain, and is independent of $i,F,S,T,j$, the sampling measure, and the tile shift. Consequently, along any sequence with $T\\to\\infty$ and $S/T\\to0$, for every fixed $j$, averaged over $T/2\\le k\\le3T/4$, revealing $U_S$ changes the conditional law of $K_{k+j}$, given $(Q_F,K_k)$, by $o(1)$ in expected $\\ell^1$ distance.         "exact_quote": "Almost surely, activity is transitively closed: $$a_{pq}(\\omega)a_{qr}(\\omega)\n       \\bigl(1-a_{pr}(\\omega)\\bigr)=0\n       \\qquad(p,q,r\\text{ distinct}).$$ Let $b\\in L$ be regular, let $V$ be the pattern group of all $b$-positive roots, and let $V_\\omega^+$ be the subgroup generated by the active $b$-positive root groups.         "exact_quote": "Fix an interior scale $R_i$ allowed in the long-wall comparison of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:plaque-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:plaque-comparison\">16</a>, with ratio-separated depths on both sides of it inside $(l_{-,i},l_{+,i})$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-partition",
        "exact_quote": "Given a sufficiently small identity neighborhood $O\\subset G$, there is a countable Borel partition $\\mathcal P$ of $X$ with the following properties.*\n\n1.  *Every atom has $\\mu$-null boundary and lies in an inner part of a relatively compact $VA$ plaque chart from the fixed stock. The chart extends beyond the atom with a margin.*\n\n2.  *If $x,y$ have the same symbols at times $0,\\ldots,N$, there are relative lifts $h_t\\in O$, $0\\leq t\\leq N$, such that $$ya(tb)=xa(tb)h_t,\n      \\qquad h_{t+1}=a(-b)h_ta(b).$$*\n\n\n3.  *For every fixed $C$, the entropies $H_\\nu(\\mathcal P)$ are bounded uniformly for all sufficiently large $i$ and all probabilities $\\nu\\leq C\\mu_i$. Moreover, there are finite coarsenings $\\mathcal P^{(q)}$, obtained by keeping finitely many atoms and combining the others into one atom, for which $$\\label{eq:partition-entropy-tails}\n      \\lim_{q\\to\\infty}\\limsup_{i\\to\\infty}\n      \\sup_{\\nu\\leq C\\mu_i}\n      H_\\nu(\\mathcal P\\mid\\mathcal P^{(q)})=0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-015.3"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-entropy-partition",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-inactive-cell-concentration",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many atoms $S$ of $\\mathcal P$, with their $VA$ charts, and an interior transverse depth $l_i\\prec R_i$. Suppose that probabilities $\\nu_i\\leq C\\mu_i$ satisfy $\\int A_{J,i}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\nu_i\\to0$. Condition on one of these symbols $S$ and on its transverse $q$ cell. Draw two points independently from the resulting conditional probability of $\\nu_i$. Then their $z$ coordinates differ by a quantity tending to zero in probability, averaged with the probabilities of the conditioning events.*\n\n*The conclusion also holds on average over any finite list of such probabilities, with arbitrary probability weights, if their weighted mean of $\\int A_{J,i}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\nu_i$ tends to zero.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-full-activity",
        "exact_quote": "For every ordered pair $j\\ne k$ in $\\{1,\\ldots,6\\}$, $$\\label{eq:full-activity}\n \\int_X g_{jk,i}\\,\\mathop{}\\!d\\mu_i\\longrightarrow1.$$ Consequently every root is active almost surely in the limiting state law.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-root-line-laws",
        "exact_quote": "For any such convergent subsequence, the limiting law and its prelimit arrays have the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *Almost surely, for every $v_0\\in L$ with rational coordinates, every oriented cut $J$, and every positive rational $\\tau$, $$\\label{eq:positive-cut-integral}\n      \\sum_{\\beta\\in\\mathcal E_J}\\int_0^\\tau\n      F_\\beta\\bigl(\\beta(v_0+t b_J)\\bigr)\\,\\mathop{}\\!dt>0.$$*\n\n\n2.  *For distinct $j,k,\\ell$, and every bounded rectangle $B\\subset\\mathbb R^2$, the nonnegative defects satisfy $$\\label{eq:line-transitivity-defect}\n      \\mathbb E_{x\\sim\\mu_i}\\int_B\n      G_{jk,i}^{x}(s)G_{k\\ell,i}^{x}(t)\n      \\bigl(1-G_{j\\ell,i}^{x}(s+t)\\bigr)\\,\\mathop{}\\!ds\\,\\mathop{}\\!dt\n      \\longrightarrow0.$$",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **primitive field** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A degree-six number field $K$ is *primitive* if there is no field $F$ with $\mathbb Q\subsetneq F\subsetneq K$.
- **codifferent** ([source](#pm-h-11)): Let $\mathfrak D_K$ be the different of $K$, so $$\mathfrak D_K^{-1} =\{z\in K:\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}_{K/\mathbb Q}(z\mathcal O_K)\subset\mathbb Z\}.$$
- **oriented cut** ([source](#sec:segment-entropy)): An *oriented cut* is a nonempty proper subset $J\subset\{1,\ldots,6\}$, with the roots directed from $J$ to its complement:

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-packet-volume** [claim](#stmt-prop-packet-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-packet-volume): Unit-theorem volume count and unfolding over ideals -> unfolding identity
- **step-ideal-estimates** [claim](#stmt-lem-ideal-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ideal-estimate): Zero-free region then Shiu-type short-interval bound -> uniform ideal counts
- **step-cusp** [claim](#stmt-prop-cusp) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-cusp): Count short vectors through the unfolding -> tightness
- **step-ball** [claim](#stmt-prop-ball) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ball): Marked vector in local charts and ideal count -> ball bound
- **step-trace-slice** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-slice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-slice): Dual basis and transference of minima -> slice count
- **step-tube** [claim](#stmt-prop-bowen) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bowen): Count ordinary and dual vector pairs through codifferent products -> tube decay
- **step-positive-entropy** [claim](#stmt-prop-positive-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-positive-entropy): Cover tubes by balls and apply an entropy criterion -> entropy bound
- **step-ekl** [claim](#stmt-thm-ekl) / [proof](#stmt-thm-ekl): Cited measure classification -> homogeneous components
- **step-block-groups** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-groups) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-groups): Root graph with unimodularity balance -> block structure
- **step-fixed-pieces** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-pieces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-pieces): Commutant field and unit of infinite order -> countable fixed pieces
- **step-no-periods** [claim](#stmt-lem-no-periods) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-no-periods): Poincare recurrence under contraction -> embedded plaques
- **step-plateau** [claim](#stmt-lem-plateau) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plateau): Bounded martingale increments over logarithmic depths -> stable depths
- **step-sampling** [claim](#stmt-lem-sampling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-sampling): Pass conditional identity to the limit -> sampling identity
- **step-patch-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-patch-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-patch-comparison): Sum mass errors over trimmed patches -> patch comparison
- **step-plaque-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-plaque-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-plaque-comparison): Fit images into output charts, trim, cross-multiply -> projective plaque measures
- **step-u-factor** [claim](#stmt-lem-u-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-u-factor): Contraction and patch comparison -> provisional product
- **step-tiles** [claim](#stmt-lem-tiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tiles): Digit expansion tile for the contraction -> nested tiles
- **step-stage-information** [claim](#stmt-lem-stage-information) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stage-information): Chain rule for mutual information and Pinsker -> label information bound
- **step-product-rule** [claim](#stmt-thm-product) / [proof](#stmt-thm-product): Contract for the root factor, expand tiles for the normal factor -> product formula
- **step-activity** [claim](#stmt-prop-activity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-activity): Iterate the product rule and compare Heisenberg factorizations -> transitive activity
- **step-va-support** [claim](#stmt-lem-va-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-va-support): Product decomposition from local diagonal invariance -> support on active group times diagonal
- **step-activity-indicators** [claim](#stmt-lem-activity-indicators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-activity-indicators): Continuity-set approximation of activity -> sequence indicators
- **step-transverse-inactive** [claim](#stmt-lem-transverse-inactive) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transverse-inactive): Atom at identity plus dilation invariance -> inactivity on countable sections
- **step-entropy-partition** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-partition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-partition): Layered cusp partition with consistent lifts -> orbit-name partition
- **step-inactive-concentration** [claim](#stmt-lem-inactive-cell-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inactive-cell-concentration): Support lemma and change of measure -> concentration in cross coordinate
- **step-segment-activity** [claim](#stmt-prop-segment-activity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inactive-cell-concentration-2): Compare lower tube entropy bound with upper mesh coding bound -> positive segment activity
- **step-root-line-laws** [claim](#stmt-lem-root-line-laws) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-root-line-laws): Wall invariance and segment activity along root lines -> limiting root functions
- **step-deterministic-arrays** [claim](#stmt-lem-deterministic-root-arrays) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-deterministic-root-arrays): Markov selection of arrays near the limit -> deterministic arrays
- **step-positive-intervals** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-root-intervals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-root-intervals): Strongly connected root graph and composition along a path -> positivity on intervals
- **step-convolution** [claim](#stmt-lem-convolution) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-convolution): Uniform local limit estimate via characteristic functions -> uniform lower bound
- **step-full-activity** [claim](#stmt-thm-full-activity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-convolution-2): Two-edge cycles and repeated convolution remove inactive sets -> all roots active
- **step-main-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:assembly): Exclude proper homogeneous components using full activity -> Haar limit

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-haar-limit-no-escape**: For totally real primitive sextic fields with discriminants tending to infinity and any choice of embedding orderings, the complete volume-weighted packet probabilities converge weakly to Haar probability on the space of unimodular lattices. No mass escapes: every tolerance is met by one compact set for all large indices.
- **inv-ball-mass-bound**: Every weak limit of the packet probabilities gives balls of small radius a mass bounded by a constant times the sixth power of the radius, uniformly for centres in a compact set.
- **inv-packet-volume**: Each orbit of a packet has diagonal-group volume given by half the sign index times the regulator, and the number of distinct orbits is fixed by class number, sign index and the power of two stated.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-imprimitive-fields**: Apply the equidistribution statement to sextic fields that contain an intermediate subfield. -> The argument excludes block-diagonal homogeneous limits using primitivity; with an intermediate field the packets may concentrate near such proper orbits and no Haar limit is claimed.
- **anti-single-orbit**: Sample a single orbit or one ideal class and expect Haar-distributed lattices. -> The theorem concerns the whole volume-weighted packet; one orbit carries no stated equidistribution.
- **anti-fixed-discriminant**: Use the theorem for a specific field of moderate discriminant and read off an error bound. -> The statement is a limit as the discriminant tends to infinity and gives no rate or threshold.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are asymptotic and existential limit statements obtained from measure rigidity, entropy and conditional-measure arguments (equidistribution as discriminants tend to infinity, with weak limits taken along subsequences). No finite procedure with explicit inputs and outputs is stated, so no runnable reference implementation with an independent oracle can certify them.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an asymptotic equidistribution statement as discriminants tend to infinity. It gives no executable procedure with a stated interface, so there is no library routine to extract. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the result concerns simulation dynamics or procedural generation. The limit is a statement about weak convergence of measures on the space of unimodular lattices. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computational tool, checker or verifier is described. The proof proceeds through measure classification and entropy arguments that are not mechanised. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ideal-count and tube estimates are analytic upper bounds inside a limiting argument, not steps of an algorithm. Convergence is asymptotic, with no stated rate or finite procedure. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper defines no data structure. Packets of orbits and nested tiles are analytic devices for proofs, not representations designed for storage or lookup. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no computational kernel or arithmetic circuit in the paper to accelerate, and no resource or throughput claim. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Ideal lattices appear here only as a source of orbits in the space of lattices. No hardness assumption, security reduction or scheme is considered, and the equidistribution statement gives no security guarantee. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No learning problem, model or training procedure is involved. Conditional probabilities in the proof are measure-theoretic objects and not estimators. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no objective function, constraint system or search procedure. The entropy lower bound is a structural property of limit measures rather than an optimisation result. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no concurrency, communication or consensus content. Its sums over ideal classes are number-theoretic, not parallel computations. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result is pure mathematics: it shows that limits of packets are Haar measure for totally real primitive sextic fields, via rigidity of diagonal actions. It is not adapted to engineering use, and the block-subgroup exclusion is specific to this degree. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **lim-primitivity-hypothesis** (hypothesis): The theorem needs every field in the sequence to be primitive, with no field strictly between the rationals and the sextic field. Primitivity is what separates the dual-lattice slice and excludes the block-diagonal limits. Blocked use: ['Applying the equidistribution conclusion to sextic fields that contain a quadratic or cubic subfield', 'Treating packets of imprimitive fields as converging to Haar probability']
- **lim-totally-real-degree-six** (hypothesis): The fields are totally real of degree six, so the full split diagonal group acts on the lattices. The result is stated for the degree-six case. Blocked use: ['Using the result for fields with complex places', 'Transferring the statement to other degrees without redoing the degree-specific block analysis']
- **lim-discriminant-asymptotic** (formal_scope): The conclusion is a limit as the discriminants tend to infinity. No rate of convergence and no explicit discriminant threshold is stated. Blocked use: ['Reading off an error bound for a field of fixed moderate discriminant', 'Claiming near-Haar behaviour of a packet at a specific finite discriminant']
- **lim-complete-packet-only** (hypothesis): The measure is built from the whole packet, with all sign translates and all ideal classes weighted by orbit volume. Nothing is claimed about an individual orbit. Blocked use: ['Sampling one orbit or one ideal class and expecting Haar-distributed lattices', 'Dropping the sign translates or the volume weights']
- **lim-subsequence-limit-nonconstructive** (nonconstructive): The identification of the limit proceeds by taking weak subsequential limits and arguing about their components, not by a finite procedure. The main assembly gives no algorithm. Blocked use: ['Extracting a computable approximation scheme or running-time bound from the proof', 'Presenting the equidistribution result as a constructive algorithm']
- **lim-tube-time-window** (approximation): The exponential tube decay holds only for large indices and only for times inside the growing interval between the two stated time scales. The arithmetic stage gives no control outside that window. Blocked use: ['Applying the tube bound at times below the lower scale or above the upper scale', 'Using the decay at a fixed index without enlarging the discriminant threshold']
- **lim-sextic-setting-standing** (formal_scope): The dynamical stages assume the standing convention that the dimension is six, with the diagonal group of the six-dimensional special linear group acting on the right. Constants may depend on fixed compact sets and charts. Blocked use: ['Citing the conditional-measure and product-rule stages in another dimension without rechecking the root and block structure', 'Treating the implicit constants as uniform over all compact sets']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that the complete ideal-class packets of maximal orders in totally real sextic fields with no proper intermediate fields become equidistributed, with no escape of mass, in the space of unimodular lattices as their field discriminants tend to infinity. The limit is Haar probability measure. Each packet includes all coordinate-sign translates and is weighted by diagonal-orbit volume.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The real embeddings of a totally real number field turn its ideals into lattices. Multiplication by positive diagonal matrices changes the relative sizes of the embedding coordinates while preserving covolume. The units make each resulting diagonal orbit compact, and the ideal classes assemble these orbits into a finite packet. The distribution of such packets connects the arithmetic of number fields with the dynamics of higher-rank diagonal groups. We prove equidistribution for totally real sextic fields having no proper intermediate field.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Packets and the main theorem

Let $$X_6=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_6(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_6(\mathbb R),\qquad
 A_6=\{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(e^{u_1},\ldots,e^{u_6}):\textstyle\sum_j u_j=0\}.$$ We regard $X_6$ as the space of covolume-one lattices in $\mathbb R^6$, with matrices acting on row vectors on the right. Let $m_6$ denote its $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_6(\mathbb R)$-invariant probability. We normalize Haar measure on $A_6$ as $\mathop{}\!du_1\cdots\mathop{}\!du_5$, with $u_6=-\sum_{j=1}^5u_j$.

A degree-six number field $K$ is *primitive* if there is no field $F$ with $\mathbb Q\subsetneq F\subsetneq K$. Suppose $K$ is totally real, choose an ordering $\sigma=(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_6)$ of its real embeddings, and write $\mathcal O_K$ for its ring of integers. For a nonzero fractional $\mathcal O_K$-ideal $I$, set $$\sigma(I)=\{(\sigma_1(d),\ldots,\sigma_6(d)):d\in I\},\qquad
 \Lambda_{I,\sigma}=\mathop{\mathrm{covol}}(\sigma(I))^{-1/6}\sigma(I).$$ Choose one representative of each ordinary ideal class. Let $\mathcal P_{K,\sigma}$ be the set of distinct compact orbits $$\Lambda_{I,\sigma}wA_6,
 \qquad w=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\epsilon_1,\ldots,\epsilon_6),\quad
 \epsilon_j\in\{1,-1\}.$$ Here every sign matrix acts on the underlying lattice, including those of determinant $-1$. Dirichlet’s unit theorem gives compactness of the orbits. Including all signs makes the packet independent of the chosen ordinary ideal-class representatives.

For $\mathcal O\in\mathcal P_{K,\sigma}$, let $m_{\mathcal O}$ be its invariant probability and $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_A(\mathcal O)$ its volume for the specified Haar measure. The packet probability is <a id="eq:packet-definition"></a>$$\label{eq:packet-definition}
 \mu_{K,\sigma}=
 \frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal O\in\mathcal P_{K,\sigma}}
              \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_A(\mathcal O)m_{\mathcal O}}
      {\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal O\in\mathcal P_{K,\sigma}}
              \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_A(\mathcal O)}.$$

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $K_i$ be totally real primitive fields of degree six, with $|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K_i)|\to\infty$, and choose any ordering $\sigma_i$ of the embeddings of each field. Then $$\int_{X_6} f\,\mathop{}\!d\mu_{K_i,\sigma_i}
       \longrightarrow\int_{X_6} f\,\mathop{}\!dm_6
       \qquad(f\in C_c(X_6)).$$ There is no escape of mass: for every $\epsilon>0$ there is a compact $C\subset X_6$ such that $\mu_{K_i,\sigma_i}(C)\ge1-\epsilon$ for all sufficiently large $i$.*

</div>

The theorem concerns each complete packet attached to a field. It allows the fields and their embedding orderings to vary freely under the stated hypotheses. The arithmetic role of primitivity is to make every nonrational integer of $K_i$ generate the full field; this will yield uniform separation in the dual lattice used to count thin tubes.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### History and the composite-degree obstruction

For real quadratic fields, the corresponding packets project to packets of closed geodesics on the modular surface. Linnik’s ergodic method, including Skubenko’s treatment of the one-sheeted hyperboloid, gave equidistribution under an auxiliary splitting condition (Linnik 1968, vol. 45, chap. VI) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Linnik1968](#ref-Linnik1968)]; see also the historical statements in (Einsiedler et al. 2012, Theorems 1.1–1.2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[ELMV2012](#ref-ELMV2012)]. Duke removed this condition for fundamental discriminants by analytic methods (Duke 1988, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Duke1988](#ref-Duke1988)]. Einsiedler, Lindenstrauss, Michel, and Venkatesh later gave a dynamical proof formulated for all positive nonsquare order discriminants (Einsiedler et al. 2012, Theorem 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ELMV2012](#ref-ELMV2012)]. In higher degree the compact diagonal orbits have dimension greater than one, and the dynamics of the full diagonal group provides additional rigidity.

Einsiedler, Katok, and Lindenstrauss proved that, on $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_d(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_d(\mathbb R)$ for $d\ge3$, an ergodic probability invariant under the full positive diagonal group and having positive entropy for a diagonal flow is algebraic (Einsiedler, Katok, and Lindenstrauss 2006, Theorem 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[EKL2006](#ref-EKL2006)]. Einsiedler, Lindenstrauss, Michel, and Venkatesh developed the connection between arithmetic separation of periodic torus orbits and entropy of their limiting measures (Einsiedler et al. 2009) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ELMV2009](#ref-ELMV2009)]. Their subsequent cubic equidistribution theorem combined measure classification, subconvexity, and local harmonic analysis (Einsiedler et al. 2011) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[ELMV2011](#ref-ELMV2011)]. These works distinguish two tasks that remain central here: establishing nonescape and entropy, and identifying the algebraic components of a limit.

The second task is particularly important in composite degree (Einsiedler et al. 2011, sec. 1.6.3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[ELMV2011](#ref-ELMV2011)]. In prime degree, the positive-entropy classification gives Haar measure directly (Einsiedler, Katok, and Lindenstrauss 2006, Corollary 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[EKL2006](#ref-EKL2006)]. In degree six there are proper block subgroups containing the full diagonal group whose closed orbits can carry finite invariant measures. Primitivity of the fields in the sequence does not, by itself, rule out concentration near such orbits. The proof must retain enough information from the sequence to exclude these limiting components.

Subsequent work has sharpened the entropy estimates in higher degree. Khayutin obtained bounds for maximal-order packets whose Galois closures act two-transitively on the embeddings (Khayutin 2019, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Khayutin2019](#ref-Khayutin2019)]. Wieser and Yang obtained entropy bounds for quartic packets of maximal type with quadratic subfields, under a fixed split-place condition and additional discriminant-growth hypotheses (Wieser and Yang 2026) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[WieserYang2026](#ref-WieserYang2026)]. These results clarify the arithmetic information available in composite degree; identifying every component of a limit remains a separate step in the argument here.

The arithmetic preparation adapts the ordinary-vector method used for prime-degree packets (OpenAI 2026a, secs. 2–6) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[PrimeDegreePackets](#ref-PrimeDegreePackets)] and primitive quartic packets (OpenAI 2026b, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[QuarticPackets](#ref-QuarticPackets)]. We give the needed estimates in full. They use Stark’s effective zero-free information (Stark 1974) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Stark1974](#ref-Stark1974)] and the short-interval bounds of Shiu and Pollack (Shiu 1980; Pollack 2020) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[Shiu1980](#ref-Shiu1980), [Pollack2020](#ref-Pollack2020)]. The quartic work treats its additional obstruction using exterior-square arithmetic. Here an ordinary vector and a dual vector produce a codifferent element, and primitivity gives the separation needed to estimate its partial traces.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### The intermediate-resolution argument

The main additional argument concerns conditional measures that retain information at growing, but separated, resolutions. We describe its purpose before introducing the construction.

Let $\mu_i=\mu_{K_i,\sigma_i}$. Ordinary-vector estimates prove tightness and give every probability limit $\mu$ an $O(r^6)$ bound for the mass of radius-$r$ balls, uniformly when their centers range over a compact set. Since $A_6$ has dimension five, this bound forces positive entropy in almost every $A_6$-ergodic component. Theorem <a href="#thm:ekl" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:ekl">9</a> then makes those components homogeneous. Every proper component is supported on points fixed by a nonidentity diagonal matrix with algebraic entries, so only a countable family of fixed-point sets must be excluded.

The arithmetic estimate used for this exclusion concerns two-sided tubes, with $O$ a sufficiently small identity neighborhood, $$x\bigl(a(tb)Oa(-tb)\cap a(-tb)Oa(tb)\bigr),
 \qquad a(b)=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(e^{b_1},\ldots,e^{b_6}),\quad\sum_jb_j=0.$$ If the coordinates of $b$ are separated across a prescribed cut of the indices, ordinary and dual vectors bound the packet mass of such a tube by $e^{-\eta t}$ for a fixed $\eta>0$. The estimate holds throughout a growing interval $[T_{-,i},T_{+,i}]$ with $T_{+,i}/T_{-,i}\to\infty$. The breadth of this interval is used throughout the proof.

For $j\ne k$, let $U_{jk}=\{1+sE_{jk}:s\in\mathbb R\}$. Exact conditional measures of a finite packet along these root groups are supported at a single point. They therefore cannot express the spread detected by the arithmetic tube bound. Instead, in a local box $(q,h)$ for a unipotent group, we condition the plaque coordinate $h$ on a small cell of the transverse coordinate $q$. We choose logarithmic cell depths inside the growing interval so that the conditional probabilities change negligibly across a much wider internal range. In their joint limits with the sampled lattice, the plaque point still has the recorded probability as its conditional law, given the transverse coordinate and that one probability. This sampling identity will detect atoms on sets meeting each root plaque in only countably many points. The limiting probabilities also retain their behavior under suitable long diagonal motions.

Product structure for genuine leafwise measures and the use of noncommuting root foliations are basic ingredients of the higher-rank entropy method of Einsiedler and Katok (Einsiedler and Katok 2003, Proposition 8.3) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[EK2003](#ref-EK2003)] <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[EK2005](#ref-EK2005)] (Einsiedler and Katok 2005, Theorems 8.4–8.5); see also (Einsiedler and Lindenstrauss 2010, Corollary 8.8 and Theorem 9.8) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[EL2010](#ref-EL2010)]. The point here is to prove the corresponding structure for the conditional probabilities formed before taking the limit. If a diagonal element fixes one root group $U$ and contracts a complementary unipotent subgroup $W$ generated by root groups and normal in $WU$, the limiting measure on $WU$ is the product of the limiting measures on $W$ and $U$. Contraction identifies the $U$ factor. A separate conditional-information argument shows that adding a fine $U$-cell label barely changes the law of any fixed further subdivision of a typical $W$ tile. Expanding that tile turns this comparison into one for bounded $W$ tests and identifies the $W$ factor. Both arguments are needed, and neither follows by simply disintegrating the weak limit.

Call $j\to k$ active when the resulting $U_{jk}$ measure has support beyond the identity. The product rule makes activity transitive under composition of roots. It also permits an entropy argument: if crossing roots are inactive along a positive proportion of long trajectories, then their names can be encoded using only the slowly expanding directions inside the two sides of a cut. This contradicts the tube bound. Long motions in the diagonal wall $b_j=b_k$ fixing $U_{jk}$ leave its activity unchanged. Combining the resulting one-dimensional functions of the differences $b_k-b_j$ upgrades activity along trajectories to full activity at almost every limiting state. A proper homogeneous fixed-point piece forces an inactive root and is therefore excluded.

The construction separates the arithmetic estimate from this last argument. Its potentially reusable ingredients are the comparison of conditional probabilities across a broad range of transverse depths, their product rule, and the passage from entropy bounds for long segments starting in any fixed positive proportion of a packet to full root activity.

Section <a href="#sec:arithmetic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:arithmetic">2</a> proves the arithmetic bounds, and Section <a href="#sec:homogeneous" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:homogeneous">3</a> identifies the homogeneous alternatives. Sections <a href="#sec:conditioning" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conditioning">4</a> and <a href="#sec:product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:product">5</a> construct and factor the intermediate conditional measures. Section <a href="#sec:activity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:activity">6</a> records their root-group consequences. Sections <a href="#sec:segment-entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:segment-entropy">7</a> and <a href="#sec:combination" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:combination">8</a> prove the long-segment and full-activity assertions, respectively, and Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">9</a> concludes the proof.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions

For the remainder of the paper write $n=6$, $G=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb R)$, $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb Z)$, $X=\Gamma\backslash G$, and $A=A_n$. Let $L=\{b\in\mathbb R^n:\sum_j b_j=0\}$, identified with the traceless diagonal matrices, and $a(b)=\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(e^{b_1},\ldots,e^{b_n})$. We use the right action throughout. Thus motion by $a(b)$ changes a relative displacement $h$ to $a(-b)ha(b)$. Matrix neighborhoods of the identity and smooth metrics are interchangeable on fixed compact sets. When a metric on all of $X$ is needed, we use the quotient of a left-invariant Riemannian metric on $G$. The notation $B(r)$ denotes a sufficiently small identity neighborhood of radius $r$. Implicit constants may depend on fixed compact sets, charts, and $n$, but not on the varying field. All passages to subsequences preserve the probability limit under consideration.

<a id="sec:arithmetic"></a>
### Arithmetic estimates for the packets

This section establishes the three estimates on which the dynamical argument rests: uniform control of the cusp, a dimension bound for probability limits, and exponential decay on two-sided diagonal tubes. The first two follow by counting ordinary lattice vectors. For the third, we count an ordinary vector together with a dual vector; their coordinatewise product lies in the codifferent and can be counted in a thin slice.

For the volume, unfolding, and ordinary-vector estimates, we adapt the method of (OpenAI 2026a, secs. 2–6) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[PrimeDegreePackets](#ref-PrimeDegreePackets)] and (OpenAI 2026b, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[QuarticPackets](#ref-QuarticPackets)]. We reproduce the needed arguments in the packet and Haar normalizations of this paper.

Throughout this section, $K$ is a totally real field of degree six with no proper intermediate field, and $\sigma$ is an ordering of its real embeddings. Write $$D=|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K)|,\qquad Q=D^{1/2},\qquad
 \kappa=\mathop{\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_K(s),\qquad
 d_K(l)=\#\{\mathfrak b\subset\mathcal O_K:N\mathfrak b=l\}.$$ All ideals counted here are nonzero. For a sequence of fields and embedding orderings, we use the corresponding notation $$D_i=|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K_i)|,\qquad Q_i=D_i^{1/2},\qquad
 \kappa_i=\mathop{\rm Res}_{s=1}\zeta_{K_i}(s),\qquad
 \mu_i=\mu_{K_i,\sigma_i}.$$ Unless another dependence is indicated, constants below are absolute for this fixed degree. In particular, they are independent of the field and of the ordering of its embeddings.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Volume and unfolding

We first make the sign convention in the packet definition explicit. The space $X=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_6(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_6(\mathbb R)$ parametrizes ordinary unimodular lattices: a lattice has a positively oriented basis, and any two such bases differ by an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_6(\mathbb Z)$. Consequently a diagonal sign matrix acts on lattices even when its determinant is $-1$. The resulting lattice again represents a point of $X$ by choosing a positively oriented basis. There is no additional orientation coordinate.

<a id="eq:fiber-integral"></a>
Let $h_K$ and $R_K$ denote the ordinary class number and regulator, respectively. Our convention for $R_K$ is the covolume of the absolute logarithms of the units after deleting the sixth coordinate. Put $$U=\mathcal O_K^\times,\qquad
 U^+=\{\epsilon\in U:\sigma_j(\epsilon)>0\ (1\le j\le6)\},
 \qquad s=[U:U^+].$$ For a nonnegative Borel function $f$ on $\mathbb R^6$, define its lattice sum by $$\widehat f(\Lambda)=\sum_{v\in\Lambda\setminus\{0\}}f(v).$$ For $p>0$, define a logarithmic fiber integral $$\begin{aligned}
 V_f(p)
  =\sum_{\epsilon\in\{\pm1\}^6}\int_{\mathbb R^5}
  f\bigl(\epsilon_1e^{u_1},\ldots,\epsilon_5e^{u_5},
     \epsilon_6p e^{-u_1-\cdots-u_5}\bigr)
       \,\mathop{}\!du_1\cdots\mathop{}\!du_5 .                 \label{eq:fiber-integral}
\end{aligned}$$ Thus $V_f(p)$ integrates over the set on which the absolute product of the six coordinates is $p$, with the Haar normalization used for $A$.

<a id="stmt-prop-packet-volume"></a>
<div id="prop:packet-volume" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:vector-unfolding"></a>
**Proposition 2** (Packet volume and vector unfolding). *Every orbit in $\mathcal P_{K,\sigma}$ has $A$-volume $(s/2)R_K$, and there are $h_K2^6/s$ distinct orbits. In particular, the total packet volume is $Q\kappa$. For every nonnegative Borel function $f$, $$\int_X\widehat f\,\mathop{}\!d\mu_{K,\sigma}
   =\frac1{Q\kappa}\sum_{l\ge1}d_K(l)V_f(l/Q),
                                                        \label{eq:vector-unfolding}$$ where both sides are allowed to be infinite.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-packet-volume"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-packet-volume</summary>

*Proof.* The only roots of unity in a totally real field are $1$ and $-1$. The kernel of the absolute logarithmic map on $U$ therefore has order two. Its image on $U^+$ has index $s/2$ in its image on $U$. The unit theorem now gives covolume $(s/2)R_K$ for the positive-unit logarithms in the coordinates $u_1,\ldots,u_5$.

We check that these are exactly the stabilizers and identifications in the packet. Suppose a real diagonal map sends $\sigma(I)$ onto $\sigma(J)$.
